**Terrace, stated directly, and asked not to be read into further: Bréal's woman never having a name is a deliberate wink at the misogyny prevalent in frats, which must also permeate into Bréal's own household.** Left alone, per instruction. **And: maybe they have a child by the time of Maddy's recruitment visit.** The child is a free addition against the stated economy concern — an infant isn't a character needing lines or a name, and it raises the stakes of the billet: a man training guards for extra income reads differently with a mouth to feed he didn't have when Epsilon was his whole life. Offered, not required: Maddy doing something small and clinical for the child during the visit, in her own established register (Abe's tincture, Attis, Diana's leg).

**Terrace corrected the timeline: months, not years — winter aid, then a recruitment in spring. And: Maddy could bring milk powder or a formula she concocted, or access to a wet nurse.** This walks back the earlier "post-war, years later" placement. The proximity-to-*Druzhina* worry turns out to be moot regardless of timing, since a single atomized individual selling his own off-hours labor to a private household actually *conforms* to Fleming's own atomization doctrine on its face — no body, no officer corps — so it never trips his wire. Recommended layering both offers: the formula as the acute first-visit fix, and arranging access to a wet nurse as the standing arrangement that gives Maddy a legitimate, recurring reason to be in that house across the following months — the better engine for a winter-to-spring runway, since by the time she raises the billet she isn't arriving cold. Access is also closer to the family's actual currency than a product is — standing and entry to a network, the same instrument as Nona's affiliation, Diana's leg, Lewis's place. Available, not stated: Maddy has done exactly this before, concocting something at a kitchen table with Collie, for Alice, unsuccessfully (`07-boil-water.md`) — doing it again, successfully, for a stranger's child, can sit under the scene unspoken.

**Terrace floated: Maddy nursing the child herself — not sexual, but woman's-body-as-site-of-politics, partly to make the ask for Bréal's contribution to House Swartz practically undeniable, taking after Collie's own no-holds-barred politicking, including milk-kinship.** **Terrace then stressed, heavily and repeatedly: this is in the much later chapters — when Maddy is a bona fide doctor, and, more importantly, not a minor.**

Fully endorsed the timing constraint, not merely accepted it — the device needs an adult exercising it on her own initiative to read as her chosen escalation of the family method rather than something done to her, and the credential matters separately from the age: understanding exactly what she's trading. Grounded the device itself: **induced milk-kinship is a real, historically documented institution**, consistent with the book's method of using real institutions rather than inventing customs (*druzhina*, *devşirme*, *krugovaya poruka*, the *annona* are the same move), and specifically legible against *radha'a*'s mahram status and Ottoman *süt kardeşliği*. Tied it to Maddy's already-established body-focused arc (Diana's stump, Carmen's pregnancy, the unstated spotting in *Mulberry*) as a logical last stop on an existing line, and to the Medea myth Collie gave her in *Boil Water* — a girl who *"made magic and medicine,"* finally choosing to wield the craft rather than having it narrated to her. **Named the thematic closure this offers back to the session's opening question:** if Collie converts and never produces, and Maddy — her chosen heir on the caregiving side — eventually has a child and turns the literal fact of that production into her sharpest political tool, that's the generation Collie trained finally doing the one thing Collie's own method structurally cannot. Two open decisions flagged, unresolved: whether the maneuver requires Maddy to have her own child, and whether it lands back on the same Bréal household decades later or on a fresh subject entirely.

**Terrace offered a mechanism refinement: Maddy could provide ante- and post-natal care to Bréal's household as a favour to an old friend of the Swartzes, and asked whether induced lactation without a prior pregnancy is real and historically old.**

Confirmed factually — induced lactation is real and ancient. The mechanism is sustained stimulation triggering prolactin, with no pregnancy required, and it appears in the historical and legal record specifically around milk-kinship: classical Islamic jurisprudence on *radha'a* directly debates whether milk produced without pregnancy still creates the same kinship. Endorsed the reframing as mechanically superior to the earlier options: it removes the need to establish Maddy's own motherhood as a separate canon fact, and it makes the act more deliberately premeditated — chosen weeks or months ahead of a birth she already knows is coming, with nothing biological forcing her hand, a sharper image of "no-holds-barred" than incidental co-nursing would be. The antenatal-care framing is the deniable layer the device needs, matching the book's established method (Nadia's chronometer argument, Collie's cake) — completely true, not the account that matters. **Named the direct payoff to existing text:** *Boil Water*'s *"I was there when you were born and I was there with you when we buried her"* is literally how Maddy became a Swartz. If she runs the identical sequence — presence at a birth, followed by a deliberate claim on the child — on someone else's child, consciously, as a tool rather than as grief, that is the family's founding move performed a second time by the person it was originally performed on, done this time with her eyes open.

**Terrace named the thread: Doula Diplomacy.** Endorsed as a working title for the notes; flagged that the manuscript's own naming register runs cooler (*Ground*, *Mulberry*, *Formulary*) than an alliterative pun, so the eventual chapter title may want to sit closer to that. Noted, available whether stated on the page or not: *doula* is Greek for female slave — not a coincidence worth wasting, given the same session's servants-and-retainers material. A woman using her own body's labor to bind a family to hers, under a word whose root is bondage, in a book where Collie teaches the classics that word comes from.

### Timeline pinned — Terrace, session of 2026-09-02

**Year zero is 2023.** Collie's bus scene (ch0 ¶1) is the early collapse era; the Christmas where Maia and Alex come to the door is a crisis point years later, not the initial event. The collapse is a process, not a single day.

Children are born post-collapse or at its onset: Lewis first (~2025–2027), then Nadia, then Maddy three months after Nadia.

**The current movement of the written chapters is approximately 2044** — roughly twenty-one years post-collapse. This is Terrace's call.

The "twenty years ago" line in `10-wild-oats.md:59` ("would have been classified as a Destructive Device and bankrupted the university in tax stamps") points back to ~2024, when ATF regulations were in force. It does not measure the age of the collapse — it measures how long ago the regulatory world existed. The line works as written.

**Implication for the technological landscape:** at 21 years, the polity is at the transition between salvage economy and local manufacture. Most smartphones are dead. Laptops and desktops survive through institutional maintenance and cannibalization but the fleet shrinks every year. The landline and radio networks are the durable communication systems. Vehicles run on biodiesel and are maintained at the cost of increasing parts scarcity. Pharmaceuticals are locally produced from plant stock, not stockpiled pills — the pharmacopoeia greenhouse is load-bearing for this reason.

### Collapse logistics and technology timeline — provisional canon, same session

**The collapse is regional and gradual, not global and sudden.** The West Coast (or wherever the university sits) leads the decline. Global manufacturing — especially phone and electronics production in Asia (China, India, Vietnam, Ethiopia) — continues well into the 2030s, serving domestic and developing-world markets that never depended on the US. Trade routes to the university's area degrade but persist as informal channels: ships that still dock, border crossings, overland crates. The polity is *cut off*, not post-apocalyptic.

**Provisional calendar:**

| Year | What happens |
|---|---|
| **~2023** | Year zero. Collie on the bus. Early collapse — infrastructure underinvestment, institutional erosion, the gas price that begins with a two. Not an event but a slope. |
| **~2025–2028** | Children born. Lewis first, then Nadia, then Maddy three months later. Collie rises through TA → Adjunct → Senior Lecturer. Old-world retail still operates but prices climb and selection thins. |
| **~2030–2033** | Nadia's childhood block (ch0 ¶17–19). Buses still run in two colors. Supermarkets open. Daycare exists. Alice is alive and working at the university. The world is declining but functioning. Formal imports slow; off-brand and collapse-era devices (simpler chipsets, repairable designs) replace flagship products on shelves. |
| **~2035–2038** | Lewis at twelve (ch0 ¶23). Lights going out at Alex's house. The old house sold. Infrastructure visibly failing. Grid power with load shedding. Electronics retailers still receive somewhat-reliable imports of simpler, collapse-era devices — phones built on older process nodes by Asian manufacturers serving surviving markets. |
| **~2036–2038** | Collapse Christmas (ch0 ¶75). Maia and Alex at the door. International students leave and don't come back. This is a crisis intensification, not the start. Laminated cards written. |
| **~2036–2039** | Formal retail dies. The Best Buy (or equivalent) closes — inventory exhausted, import routes unreliable, barter economy can't sustain fixed retail. Last phones to arrive were manufactured ~2033–2036: simpler, repairable, collapse-era designs on older chipsets. Display units and unsold stock sit in back rooms. |
| **~2041–2043** | Ch1 present. Lewis at the shop, convoy runs with Bréal. The hardware store in the city still has staff, a warehouse, and truck deliveries — but "the trucks will be going elsewhere next month." Fixer brings in liquor reliably, chips spottily. Frames printed when they can't be bought. The display-unit phone jailbroken — manufactured ~2033–2036, five to eight years old, battery degraded but functional. The salvage economy is contracting. |
| **~2043–2044** | Ch7–Ch11 present (Boil Water through Mulberry). Maddy at 14–16. Wild Oats convoy. Druzhina. The polity has finished improvising and started institutionalizing. |

**Manuscript anchors for the supply chain:** `01-continuity-test.md:15` — "The trucks will be going elsewhere next month" (store still receiving deliveries, route about to shift). `:1` — "Fixer was reliable with bringing in liquor and spotty with chips" (informal importer with variable supply, not salvage). `:1` — "new frames were printed instead of bought" (imports still exist as an option, printing is workaround for unreliable supply). `:153` — "two shipping boxes of laptops and speakers and headphones" (retail-packaged goods, possibly from store stock or late shipments).

**Key implication for the Swartz naval pivot:** Nadia's hemp → cordage → ships is not only a local economic strategy but a route back to global trade. A ship can reach a port where Asian-manufactured goods are still available. The chronometer Lewis and Ada can build is what makes that ship navigate. The polity's isolation is geographic and logistical, not civilisational — and the naval house is the one that can break it.

### The satellite re-entry — Terrace, session of 2026-09-02

**Terrace's pitch:** a chapter (or scene) where the narrator narrates a satellite failing part by part, eventually burning through the atmosphere. Registered on the ground between the lovers Ada and Nadia as a shooting star among a now very starry sky, as they lie on overgrown grass in a stolen romantic moment.

**The object:** a polar-orbiting weather satellite (NOAA-type) at ~800 km, launched ~2019–2021, now twenty-plus years past design life. Not GPS (too high to re-enter in the story's timeframe) and not Starlink (gone years ago). Polar orbit is correct because it crosses every latitude (visible from anywhere) and is low enough for atmospheric drag to bring it down within the story's century.

**The failure sequence (technical):** Reaction wheel bearings seized years ago. The satellite tumbles, solar panels catching sunlight at random angles. The transmitter has been silent for a decade. The onboard computer is stuck in a boot loop — restarts in sunlight, crashes in shadow. At 780 km, upper-atmosphere drag is slight but cumulative. Perigee drops a few hundred meters per orbit. Each lower pass means denser air, more drag, lower perigee — the decay accelerates itself. On the last night, below 200 km, the aluminum skin heats. Solar panels rip away first (designed for vacuum, not air). The titanium fuel tank survives longest. Forty-five seconds from first glow to gone.

**What they see:** a streak longer and slower than a meteor, breaking into fragments partway through. Color shifts white to orange as different materials burn. No sound. Maybe thirty degrees of arc. Against a sky with no light pollution, Milky Way edge to edge — the darkness is beautiful because the grid is dark.

**Structural question:** the satellite's internal failure sequence is invisible from the ground. If the narrator describes it, the narration goes omniscient for this passage — a register the book hasn't used. The alternative: the technical sequence is implied by what the characters see (too slow for a meteor, wrong direction for a known shower, fragments) and what Ada quietly identifies. The second option costs less and lands harder.

**Character pressures:** Ada is a computer and would know the streak is not a meteor — wrong speed, wrong trajectory. If Maia's observatory keeps a catalog of what's still in orbit, Ada might know roughly which satellite it was. The knowledge is the cost of the moment: she can't unknow it, naming it aloud changes the silence. Nadia might not know, or might not care, or might know and choose the wish anyway. "Stolen" implies they're not supposed to be here, or together, or both. The overgrown grass is a place that used to be maintained — a quad, a lawn. The beauty is a direct product of the collapse.

### The university is in Cascadia — Terrace, session of 2026-09-02

Confirmed by Terrace. The university sits in the Pacific Northwest (Oregon/Washington/British Columbia region). This is the anchor for all route calculations and trade geography.

**Immediate Cascadia resources (not voyages — politics):**

- **Puget Sound Naval Shipyard, Bremerton** — drydocks, machine shops, marine-grade steel. Days away by coastal sail, possibly overland. The industrial upgrade, if whoever holds it will deal.
- **Boeing Everett** — aerospace precision tooling and machine shops.
- **Columbia River dams (Bonneville, Grand Coulee)** — hydroelectric power that needs no fuel. A concrete dam lasts centuries; the river keeps flowing. The grid's "load shedding" (ch0 ~2037) may be partially maintained hydro with degraded transmission, not a dying grid. The EE department's most important long-term project may be repairing transmission lines, not building generators.
- **Other Cascadia universities** — Oregon State (agricultural research, seed collections), UW, UBC. Short trips, shared institutional culture.
- **Hanford** — nuclear reservation, contaminated, dangerous, but containing materials. A known hazard to the east.

### Future sailing ambitions — same session

The Pacific is the Swartzes' ocean. Cascadia faces west and south. The Atlantic requires solving Panama or Cape Horn first.

**Tier 1 — Senate-fundable, near term (generation 2–3):**

| Destination | Distance | Time | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CIMMYT, Texcoco, Mexico** | ~3,000 mi coastal | 3–4 weeks | Maize and wheat genetic stock, tens of thousands of landraces. Breeding methodology archives — how to develop new crop varieties in the field without a genetics lab. Closes the same gap as Svalbard for staple crops, at a fraction of the risk. |
| **Hawaii** | ~2,400 mi open Pacific | 15–20 days | Permanent mid-Pacific trading partner and resupply point. Pre-contact Hawaiian agriculture (taro, fish ponds) may mean a community that adapted to the collapse better than continental polities. Stepping stone for everything further west. |

**Tier 2 — the big crossings (generation 3–4):**

| Destination | Distance | Time | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Japan** | ~4,500 mi North Pacific | 5–8 weeks | A marginal Japan that still manufactures: steel, electronics, tools, medicine. The Manila Galleon reborn, reversed — chronometers and hemp cordage west, manufactured goods east. Kuroshio Current and prevailing westerlies favor the return. The trade relationship that transforms the economy. |
| **Galápagos** | ~3,500 mi south then west | 4–5 weeks | Depopulated by 2120. A century of undisturbed speciation. Finch measurements and naturalist logbooks are the most valuable scientific data since the collapse. Wild species, unique biology, a controlled evolutionary experiment with a hundred-year head start. |
| **Cuba** (via Panama) | ~5,000 mi | Months | Pharmaceutical and biotech knowledge designed for resource-poor conditions — the Finlay Institute, CIGB. Drug manufacturing protocols that assume you cannot import reagents. Transforms the hospital from herbal tinctures to manufactured medicine. **Requires solving Panama first.** |

**Tier 3 — generational ambitions (generation 4–5):**

| Destination | Distance | Time | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Panama Canal** | ~4,000 mi coastal | Weeks to canal, unknown at the isthmus | The gate between oceans. Whoever holds the isthmus controls Pacific–Atlantic transit. Lock system probably non-functional by 2120; negotiation or portage with whoever lives there. Unlocks the entire Atlantic. Heart of Darkness register — up the river to find who holds the gate. |
| **Iceland** | ~8,000+ mi (via Cape Horn or Arctic) | Months | Geothermal engineering knowledge. Drilling techniques, turbine design, district heating. Cascadia sits on the Ring of Fire — the geology is right, the engineering knowledge is what's missing. Brings back energy independence. |
| **Kew Millennium Seed Bank, England** | ~10,000+ mi via Cape Horn | Months | Wild plant genetics — 2+ billion seeds from 40,000 species. Medicinal plants, timber species, pharmaceutical alkaloids. Complements Svalbard (crops) with the wild pharmacopoeia. |
| **Svalbard** | ~10,000+ mi via Cape Horn or all-Arctic | Months | The backup of the world's crop diversity. 1.1 million seed samples. The insurance policy for agriculture. |
| **Easter Island** | ~5,500 mi deep South Pacific | 6–8 weeks | The civilisational mirror — the monument to a society that collapsed from resource depletion. Navigable only by chronometer (the island is a speck). What do you write in the logbook standing in front of a Moai? |

**Pairable voyages:** CIMMYT + coastal stops on the return. Galápagos + Easter Island on a single southern expedition (scientific objective outbound, the real destination further on). Hawaii as staging for the Japan crossing.

**The thematic arc:** the family that is "not productive, not reproductive" builds the ships that retrieve the world's reproductive stock (Svalbard, CIMMYT, Kew). The machine driven FOR purpose finds its ultimate purpose in bringing back the seeds. Every piece of the naval pivot — Nadia's hemp, Lewis and Ada's chronometers, the fishing town's shipyard — points toward these missions. The chronometer finds Easter Island. The pharmacopoeia knowledge comes from Cuba. The seeds come from Svalbard. The Swartz legacy is the cargo manifest of what they brought home.

### The satellite re-entry and the radio sky — context from same session

**Shortwave radio in an uneven collapse:** surviving marginal states (India, Nigeria, Germany, Japan, Brazil) maintain shortwave broadcasting as sovereignty projection. BBC World Service, All India Radio, Voice of Nigeria — the transmitter is cheap, the signal crosses oceans, and the broadcast says "we still exist." Content: news, maritime weather, agricultural forecasts, public health, time signals. Ham operators fill gaps between institutional broadcasts. Numbers stations persist as automated intelligence infrastructure.

**Maia and the radio sky (for *Skywave*):** the shortwave dial scanned end to end draws a map of the uneven collapse — voice on this frequency means someone in West Africa is still broadcasting, silence on that frequency means whoever was there is gone. She listens for WWV's tick and hears the world instead.

**Possible off-screen nuclear exchange:** two degraded states in a resource war, India-Pakistan scale. Compatible with the manuscript IF the effects land as weather (1–2°C cooling, shortened growing seasons) and a dead trade route, never diagnosed on the ground. UV increase from ozone depletion would be felt (faster sunburns, crop UV stress, immune suppression) but attributed to "harsh sun" without atmospheric monitoring. The university might detect elevated UV with spectrometric equipment (observatory, physics lab) and infer ozone damage without knowing the cause. The greenhouse becomes more valuable because glass blocks UV-B — an advantage nobody planned.

## Collie's love-verbs, and Alice's killing haunting her — 2026-09-03

**Terrace's stress, to hold as character law: Collie loves them all — the children and the adults. She just has no verbs for it beside poison, plan, and provision.** The scheming is not in tension with the love; it is the only grammar the love has. No "but." When she plans, that is her being tender. Each verb already has its textual proof: **poison** = the poppy that puts Alice to sleep (`07-boil-water.md:87`), mercy and murder the same act at the bedside; **plan** = renaming Maddy so the university knew *"you were mine"*, eyes averted because the feeling has nowhere else to go; **provision** = greenhouse, storeroom, honey, placements — she feeds the people she cannot tell. Craft consequence: the moment Collie *states* her love she breaks, same failure as stating the feminine-apocalypse thesis. Love lives in the doing; the reader reads it off the provisioning. Guards the restraint law (spitball has this at the "nobody loses their voice" note). Risk: a reader who misses Alice's poppy reads her as the cold instrumental matriarch — Alice's deathbed is the load-bearing scene that inoculates against that, so keep that beat unmistakable (poison as pure mercy, nothing else in it) and every later plan reads back as love.

**Terrace wants Alice's killing to haunt Collie more across the story.** The one calibration that keeps it from breaking her: **haunt by capacity, not by guilt.** The killing was *right* — Alice's poppy is the proof that poison is love here, and regret would muddy that and sentimentalize the mercy. The haunt is not "I shouldn't have"; it is "I did, and I am the one who can, and now I know that about myself." Self-knowledge, not remorse. It never has to be confessed, only carried — compatible with restraint, and it keeps the mercy clean while still costing her.

**Channels for the haunt, ranked (do NOT stack — pick ~two or it becomes a guilt-parade and the composure cracks; Collie is the one character who never breaks, spitball reserves the single break for Maddy):**
1. **Maddy + the white candy — the inheritance.** Strongest, externalizes, already planted (`10-wild-oats.md:267`, "Maddy made sure of that"). Collie taught the green/white grammar; now she watches her daughter handle the instrument that killed the girl's own mother. Lever to set first: **does Maddy know Alice was killed, or think she "just went to sleep"?** Sets the whole register — dramatic irony / shared silence / a reveal that recontextualizes the makeup lesson.
2. **"Sleep" as the loaded word.** She reached for the euphemism (`:87`). Make the book's most ordinary word carry the killing — a child put to bed, a patient who "just needs to rest." Cheap, invisible, needs no stated feeling.
3. **The comma-stammer returning.** She already fractures once — *"We, found some poppy."* Let that specific fracture recur only near poppy/sleep/Alice while she stays fluent everywhere else. Returning a tell, not adding a register.
4. **The poppy row.** She grows the thing she killed with. One silent image — her hand on that row, tending or refusing to harvest. Seasonal bloom = an unnamed anniversary. Use once.

**The engine that makes it inescapable:** she's the one people come to for a good death. The greenhouse + medical training means she keeps being *asked* to do it again; every dying neighbor is Alice again. Her competence at mercy is why she can't put it down.

**The endpoint (lineage closing):** the last turn is Collie understanding that Maddy will one day do to *her* what she did to Alice — the white candy she taught her daughter to make is Collie's own death being prepared, lovingly, in advance. Makes Alice's poppy not a one-off but the first instance of a family rite. Most haunting version because it isn't tragic; it's provision, and she'd call it that.

### The proposed stichomythia — and why it does NOT go in the current *Boil Water* scene

**Terrace drafted an insert (his lines, to place LATER, not here):**
> *Sleep. / It didn't hurt. / I steamed that syringe. / and you did a good job. / You had no right. / Please. / I told her to wake up soon, for dinner. / I know. I know. / Did Grandpa and Grandma know? / Alice—your mother, she, didn't, want to break their heart. / You killed her. / She wouldn't hurt you. / And you hurt her. / I wanted to follow her. But then I had you.*
> **append to mark the slip:** *You didn't, Alice did. / I had you to raise.*

Strict Maddy/Collie alternation, untagged italics. **Enacts the restraint law correctly** — Maddy breaks (her one sanctioned break, spitball), Collie holds and only fractures at the comma-stammer. Detonation is *"I steamed that syringe." / "and you did a good job."* — Maddy as the instrument, the praise curdling.

**The pregnancy allusion in "But then I had you" is deliberate and defended (Terrace): Collie twisting her own words toward the motherhood she wanted.** It works ONLY if the page marks it as a slip — hence the append. Canon guard: **Collie must never bear a biological child (pedigree.md:56, permanent constraint); Maddy is her niece, and the whole renaming arc depends on it.** Unmarked, "I had you" reads as fact and un-writes that arc; the append (*"You didn't, Alice did." / "I had you to raise."*) turns the wish visible *as* a wish. Two open forks: (a) **who corrects** — Maddy (crueller, ties "you're not my mother" to "you killed my mother," fits the scene's momentum) or Collie self-correcting (rarer, her discipline visibly failing and recovering); (b) **"to raise" = surrender or reassertion** — end on the ethic, not the diminishment: in Collie's ledger *to raise is to have*, so the line is defiance, not retreat.

**KEY FINDING — the insert does not belong after `07-boil-water.md:87`. The current scene has already spent this material, gently:**
- **`:70` Maddy already calmly owns it** — *"She took her medicine. I helped her steam the syringes."* The insert's detonation ("I steamed that syringe" as live horror) lands on ground already flat; she said it eleven lines earlier without flinching.
- **`:76-77`** — *"we did our best, Maddy. We all did. Do some deep breaths for me, baby."* — already the gentle form of "you did a good job."
- **`:73` the two browser tabs** (children needing agency + homemade smelling salts, side by side) already do manipulation-is-love silently, and better than any line.
- **`:88+` stays tender** — *"She loved you, she loved you so much" → "It is unfair" / "Yes, it is"* → tea, the ironed shirt, *"Come help me with breakfast."* Shared grief + provision. Nobody accuses. **This Maddy already knows and is at peace.**

So the accusatory version contradicts the calm complicity at `:70` and the consolation at `:88+` — whiplash in both directions. **Recommendation: keep this early scene whole and quiet; put the stichomythia in a LATER scene, an older Maddy, when the calm curdles** — when "I steamed that syringe" stops meaning *we did our best* and starts meaning *you made me help*. Same fact, years apart, opposite charge. That IS "haunt throughout": the meaning of her own hands changing on her, and the confrontation detonating precisely *because* the early scene was so tender (the reader remembers the tea and the "good job" and watches it rot). If it must go here instead, `:88-100` (consolation/tea) have to be rewritten to absorb the rupture — can't accuse and console in one breath.

**Quiet best of what's already on the page, and the register the later break would erupt out of once:** *"the syringes were properly disinfected and stored, and it drew excellently that day."* Clinical pride sitting inside grief — she is proud of how cleanly she killed her sister — is the haunting-by-capacity already working, without a raised voice.

## Abortifacients need a chemist — the deliberate gap in the stack — 2026-09-03

**The trio is already a strong capability stack (Terrace):**
- **Lewis** — engineering, repair, locks, the placed asset (soldering iron from Alex, ch1 ¶25; shelving/lockpicking, `09-ground.md`).
- **Nadia** — brokerage, administration, navigation/rate; holds the chits and seals.
- **Maddy** — medicine, compounding, *poison* (green/white candy, `10-wild-oats.md:267`; syringes/peppermint oil for Alice, `07-boil-water.md:70-72`).

**Design call (Terrace): Maddy can compound poison in-house, but a safe/reliable abortifacient she must most likely send out to a dedicated chemist. Otherwise the story is too easy.**

**Corrects my earlier "abortifacient is downhill from poison."** Wrong the way it mattered. A *poison* only has to cross one threshold — lethal — so the margin is forgiving; any sufficient dose works. A *safe* abortifacient has to land in a window: effective on the pregnancy, sub-lethal to the mother. The narrow margin I called "the poisoner's craft" is in fact **beyond kitchen compounding** — it needs standardized potency (extraction, titration, known concentration of the active compound), i.e. a real chemist, not a compounder. So the safe version is *uphill* from poison, and that is exactly the in-world reason it gets outsourced. Killing is easy; a safe abortion is hard.

**Story function of the gap:**
- Deliberate missing piece in the stack — the family must reach *outside itself*, which manufactures alliance, dependency, and risk instead of a household that solves everything internally.
- **It is the one Swartz asset that cannot stay invisible.** Everything else they hold is deniable (an abortifacient tisane looks like tea; poison looks like candy). But sending herbs to a chemist means the raw material leaves the house, an outsider knows the business, and a trail exists. It **breaks the family's signature deniability exactly once** — which is precisely where a plot lives.
- Open slot: **who is the chemist / which house?** Possible tie to the peer houses (a chemist/apothecary house, or the university chemistry faculty). Leverage runs both ways — the chemist is someone the Swartzes owe or who owes them, and who could expose them. To be settled.

**Pennyroyal — note.** *Mentha pulegium*, the classic folk abortifacient, and **peppermint's first cousin** — same genus, same steam-distilled-oil preparation as the peppermint oil Maddy already makes for Alice (`07-boil-water.md:72`). Sits one species over on the same shelf, out of the same still. Active agent pulegone is hepatotoxic; the effective dose is near the lethal one — which is the whole reason the *safe* preparation needs the chemist, not the kitchen. Free, brutal adjacency: the tenderest object in the grief scene is one plant away from the abortifacient. Silphium is the famous extinct one they *cannot* have, if an older practitioner ever wants a plant to mourn.

## Wanted beat: a childhood fever, one generation down — 2026-09-03

**Terrace: at some point we want a kid with a childhood fever — a generation down from Maddy's.** So a child of the Maddy/Nadia/Lewis cohort, not a contemporary. *The Lacks apprentice was floated for this and withdrawn in the same breath — she is roughly contemporary with the children, absorbed as an orphan, not a generation below. Do not re-litigate; the ask is settled on the next generation.* No slot, no chapter, no names yet.

**Why the beat is worth having.** Without antibiotics, childhood fever gets its old weight back: it might be nothing, it might be the thing that takes the child, and with no lab you cannot always tell in time. That uncertainty — a parent watching a hot child, unable to know which fever this is — is the whole scene, and it needs no invention beyond what the world already has.

**Clinical shape, for whoever writes it:**
- **Fever is a symptom; the job is naming the source with no lab.** Clinical only — how the fever behaves (intermittent, sustained, spiking), what rides with it (stiff neck, cough, rash, belly pain, rigors), how long it holds. The real skill is triage: self-limiting viral (ride it out) versus bacterial walking toward sepsis.
- **Antipyretics available:** willow bark / meadowsweet (plant salicylates, aspirin's origin, growable). **Knowledge marker — salicylates in a feverish child during viral illness risk Reye's syndrome, which kills.** The trained house does NOT hand a sick kid willow-bark tea; it cools and rehydrates instead. That distinction is exactly what separates a Swartz from a folk healer, and it is free characterisation. Cinchona bark (quinine) as febrifuge/antimalarial if greenhouse-grown or imported. Salvaged acetaminophen/ibuprofen mostly expired by 2044 — a few hoarded doses for the worst nights.
- **The actual treatment is supportive:** oral rehydration (water, salt, and **honey** for the sugar) is the single most life-saving item in the kit and costs nothing; tepid sponging, no over-bundling; and watching for danger signs early enough that acting still helps.
- **The crux:** a bacterial fever (pneumonia, kidney, septic wound) needs antibiotics, which are the scarcest thing in this world. Salvaged stock expired; local penicillin runs into **the same chemist problem as the abortifacient** — you can culture *Penicillium*, but purifying it to something safe to put in a body is not kitchen work. A trivial 2020 cure now kills.

**Pressures available, none mandatory:**
- **It puts Maddy where Collie stood.** By then Maddy is the house's medical authority — the reason the house survives. A beloved child burning, and Maddy holding the pharmacopeia, rhymes directly with Collie at Alice's bed. Whether she faces the same capacity (and whether the child lives) is the generational answer to the poppy. See the haunting note above.
- **It tests the patronage.** Getting the one scarce real antibiotic is a favour called in — and the house owes someone afterward. The fever is a plot engine, not only a grief.
- **It makes the invisible medical wealth visible.** The Swartz child survives a fever that kills the water-truck blocks' children, and nobody outside sees what that cost or who paid it.
- **Ties to *Boil Water*.** Many of these fevers are the water — typhoid is a sustained fever straight out of a contaminated well, and the title is an instruction. Where the cure may not exist, prevention *is* the medicine: you boil the water so the fever never starts.

## Ada Fleming — kinship, the medicine, and what it does to the marriage — 2026-09-03

### Settled

**Ada is the Emeritus's granddaughter. Terrace's call.** This answers the question flagged open twice — *"Is Ada the Emeritus's kin?"* at the *Druzhina* love-graph section and again in [`druzhina-elements.md`](druzhina-elements.md). The notes already wrote the consequence: **one line in *Druzhina* detonates in *Colt*, and Diana never had a chance from the moment the mead came out.**

**Ada is trans — and it never becomes text. Terrace's call.** Subtext only, by the *Mulberry* method (menarche carried by a garment soaked overnight and a liver-and-blood-sausage grocery, never named). **Three oblique elements, and they must never touch on the page:**
1. **The injection.** A scene of her injecting something for any plausible chronic condition, never specified. **The interval is the tell** — daily reads as insulin and closes the question; **weekly or fortnightly** matches none of the obvious chronic conditions and is findable on reread. One word decides it.
2. **Sterility, never explained.** The strongest half but weak alone: most readers will read ordinary infertility, which needs no subtext at all. It cannot carry the reading by itself.
3. **Fleming's signature on the supply.** The load-bearing leg, and the only one not about her body. A requisition he signs personally; a line that never gets cut. Reads at the surface as a grandfather protecting a sick granddaughter's medicine — wholly innocent — and carries the entire endocrine-buy-in argument without going near what is in the vial.

**Best single vehicle: a shortage.** Put her medicine on an allocation list during scarcity — same machinery as the drought triage and the landrace lost on a fair count — and let it *not* get cut when other things do. The reader watches a priority decision and never learns what is in it. **Its rank tells you its necessity.**

**Two failure modes.** (a) Do not let the sterility outweigh the injection, or it reads as tragedy-coding; the affirmative fact (she takes something, regularly, non-negotiably) must outweigh the absence. (b) **The surface reading must be complete and dignified alone** — a woman with a chronic condition and no children, full stop, an unremarkable whole person on first read. If the surface version does not stand by itself, the subtext is doing harm rather than work. *This also retires the "third steered daughter / engineered by her mother" framing risk, since nothing is ever stated.*

**Endocrine buy-in.** A person whose continued embodiment depends on the institution's pharmaceutical capacity is loyal in a way deeper than belief — it is not ideology, it is supply. **Ada is structurally the anti-Swartz:** the Swartz model makes people dependent on the *house* (medical access as patronage); Ada is dependent on the *university*. She is the institution's answer to the house, and she is in love with the house's heiress-presumptive.

**Fleming's motive must COINCIDE with the institutional one, never replace it.** The pharmacopoeia break (the demotion pulling Nadia off the greenhouse so no single house holds the analgesic supply) is established as correct institutionalism, "not a kindness," no villainy. **If the granddaughter becomes the *reason*, Fleming is a nepotist and the no-villain architecture collapses.** He would have done it anyway; the granddaughter means he would never have *not* done it. Unauditable, and he would never say it aloud.

**And the irony the arrangement produces: Fleming does not arrange the marriage.** That version was explicitly rejected in favour of Nadia and Lewis proposing it themselves. So his careful institutional safeguard is defeated when his granddaughter marries *into the very house he broke up*, by a plan a sixteen-year-old ran that he never saw. **Competent procedure undone by something nobody put on the form** — the book's own device, aimed at the man who most believes in the form.

**Where the children come from: by flesh or by covenant (`pedigree.md:115`).** This household has never reproduced by birth — Collie has three children and bore none, Maddy was renamed onto the roll, Lewis was annexed off his own parents' floor, the *Skywave* apprentice is an absorbed orphan. So **"three generations of a workshop" becomes three generations of covenant**, and here **apprenticeship and adoption are the same act** — the workshop reproduces by instruction. "Their children on the clockwork" requires no biology. ***Note for whoever reads the* Colt *row and the technical-lineage list: do not read "their children" as a pregnancy.***

**The two-lineage split, which removes the pressure entirely:**
- **The house's succession** — Nadia runs it, Maddy is why it survives; Lewis is structurally excluded anyway. Nothing about the title depends on his marriage.
- **The workshop's technical lineage** — Alex on mechanical, Lewis on electrical, Ada from computation into machine design, the next hands on the clockwork. Passes by training.

Neither requires anyone to give birth. **The only thing that ever wanted a womb was the old men's expectation.** Which produces the good quiet irony: if the alliance was meant to yield heirs and yields wards, **the marriage's political purpose fails completely while its private purpose succeeds perfectly.** Nadia gets exactly what she engineered; Fleming gets a child on a roll and not a Fleming grandchild, and nobody says the difference aloud — the same load-bearing fiction as a Vice-Admiral who can never be promoted.

**Live scene in who does not know.** Collie is banked as almost certainly unaware of Nadia and Ada. So she watches a marriage she considers institutionally flawless fail to produce heirs, reads it as a problem, and moves competently to fix something that is not broken — on correct criteria, with the one fact that would explain everything sitting outside her arithmetic. **The book's device pointed at the matriarch.**

### Open — floated 2026-09-03, NOT decided

**Diana bears Lewis's child and is hired to nurse it inside the Lewis/Ada household.** Terrace: *"absolutely heart-wrenching... something almost like a mini* Handmaid's."

**If taken, the version that works is employment, not compulsion.** *Handmaid's* compels by law and theology; this book has never compelled anyone — it excludes with forms and lets the correct choice cost everything. **Nobody makes Diana do this. She takes the job**, because for an unaffiliated woman with no surname the university recognises it is the best offer she will ever get: a wage, a roof, a card, and proximity to her own child. Crueller than a decree, and in register.

**The horror lives in the roll, not in anyone's feelings.** The child goes onto the household's line as Lewis and Ada's; **Diana goes onto the same roll as staff.** One form, one slot for mother, already filled — her relationship to her own child is a category error the paperwork cannot express. Nobody is cruel; nobody has to be. **The scene is a document, not a confrontation.** Historically ordinary rather than gothic: wet-nursing was a vast industry whose standard cruelty was poor women nursing rich women's infants. This inverts it — she is not separated from her child, she is **paid to attend it while having no claim on it.**

**It dovetails with the trans-Ada subtext:** a marriage that cannot produce heirs, hiring a woman who can, is structurally *necessary* rather than gratuitous. Taken alone — without that — it is pain chosen deliberately, since the covenant route already solves succession.

**The objection, and it is from Terrace's own notes.** *Lacks* was declined for Diana because *"putting Henrietta Lacks's name on a character built to be institutionally abused is digging her up for another round of it,"* and affiliating her was the fix that closed the concern. **This proposal walks straight back into it.** Not fatal — but the decision was once made that Diana should stop being the character things are done to, and this does a very large thing to her. Decide on purpose, not by momentum.

**What it kills if taken:** the *"Diana needs a clean exit"* guardrail above (already marked under challenge). The *"avoid a love triangle"* guardrail survives but needs its camera-fix restated — **point at her labour and the paperwork, never at Lewis's longing**; the moment it is about how he feels, it is soap.

**And the strongest thing in it:** Lewis's one act of agency was getting Diana **affiliated and employed**. This is the employment. **His gift becomes her trap, and he meant it kindly** — the only free thing he ever did, delivering her into the house as staff.

## The apocalyptic LinkedIn — trades that come back — 2026-09-04

**Principle, so the list stays diagnostic rather than decorative: each trade returns because ONE specific industrial input vanished.** Plastic and steel drums → cooper. Cheap clocks → knocker-up. Optical manufacture → lens grinder. Synthetic nitrogen → night soil. Electric refrigeration → ice cutter. Precedent already in the book: **Ada is a *computer* in the original sense**, and she exists because mechanical and electronic computation died. Every entry below should be traceable to a missing input the same way.

### The four best, in order

- **Cooper.** The most invisible infrastructure in a pre-plastic world — salt fish, oil, water, apples, powder, mead all ship and store in barrels, and there is no other container. Cascadia is stave country (fir, cedar, oak). **Not a craftsman: logistics.**
- **Ropewalk worker.** Rope is laid in a **quarter-mile purpose-built shed**, because that is how long the strands must run. So the trade arrives with *architecture* — **Nadia's hemp billet quietly obliges the university to build a very long, very strange building, and everyone will know what it is for.** Sailmaker sits beside it.
- **Lens grinder.** A scholar without spectacles stops being a scholar. Already implied: Alex calls Maia **"four-eyes"** (`09-ground.md`), and the same hands serve the observatory, Maddy's microscope, and the binoculars in `10-wild-oats.md:119`. **An absolute bottleneck on the polity's entire intellectual life.**
- **Knocker-up.** Paid to walk the street before dawn and tap bedroom windows with a long pole to wake workers. Real in Britain into the 1950s, and it exists *because clocks are expensive* — which, per the watch analysis, they now are. **The comedy-shaped bottom rung of the exact trade the Swartzes want to own.**

### Feedstock trades — invisible until you need metal or mortar

- **Charcoal burner.** Weeks alone in the woods tending a smouldering mound. No charcoal, no forge, no smelting.
- **Lime burner.** Quicklime for mortar, whitewash, agriculture and sanitation. **Alex's concrete needs it** (`09-ground.md`).
- **Saltmaker.** Preservation runs on salt and **Lewis already hauls it by the sack** (`01-continuity-test.md`). Wet-coast Cascadia cannot do solar salt well, so it is boiled with wood — fuel-expensive, therefore valuable, therefore traded.
- **Ice cutter / ice house keeper.** Winter lake ice cut, packed in sawdust, sold through summer. **Refrigeration as a harvest.** Upland trade — the maritime lowlands rarely freeze.
- **Tanner, and separately the currier.** `01-continuity-test.md:153` already moves five sides of leather in the convoy.
- **Millwright** (as distinct from miller) — the elite mechanic of the pre-industrial world and the direct ancestor of mechanical engineering. **Alex already is one in all but name.**

### Clerical and intellectual — the ones nobody guesses

- **Scrivener / copyist.** No photocopiers, scarce printing, so documents are copied by hand — which means **every document exists in a countable number of physical copies.** In a book made of paperwork that is a plot lever, not flavour.
- **Lector.** In Cuban cigar factories the workers **pooled their own wages** to hire someone to read newspapers and novels aloud while they worked. That is how a workforce got literature with no radio, and it could return exactly as it was.
- **Nomenclator.** The attendant whose whole job is remembering who everyone is. **Already banked in the Roman patronage vocabulary and already flagged as an office sitting unfilled** in a polity that must adjudicate affiliation.
- **Instrument maker**, distinct from watchmaker — sextants, theodolites, barometers, balances. **The naval arc cannot exist without one.**
- **Telegraph operator**, Morse-trained, per the earlier landline/telegraph analysis.

### The grim ones

- **Night soil collector.** With no synthetic nitrogen, human waste is fertiliser; in Japan and China it was bought and sold. **The other half of the fertility problem the crop-loss material opens with legumes.**
- **Knacker.** Dead animals rendered to tallow, glue, bone meal and hide. Nothing wasted, and somebody does it.
- **Resurrectionist.** A medical faculty needs cadavers and historically that was a trade. **In a polity that sorts people by affiliation, the question of *whose* bodies is answered by the roll.** Darkest item on the list and the most native to this book. Handle or leave, but do not use casually.

## The rooftop planters — what Fleming did not take — 2026-09-05

**Terrace: the Swartzes keep planters on their own rooftop, for their own poisons.** Not yet on the page. Wanted.

**Already established around it:** the rooftop exists and has purpose-built furniture on it (`11-mulberry.md:35` — the clothesline with hinges "should the family ever want to move it off the rooftop"); the household already grows outside institutional space (`10-wild-oats.md:61`, "the herbs being from the garden on fifth floor"); the apiary is a *separate, institutional* site, not domestic (`09-ground.md:157`, and *Druzhina* has Fleming granting it to the Elbakyans); and the family patch sits **inside the university greenhouse** (`07-boil-water.md`), which is precisely what Fleming pulls Nadia off.

**So the roof is the third site, and the only one outside anyone's books.**

**The consequence, and it is large: Fleming's reassignment does not disarm them.** His stated objection in `08-druzhina.md` is *"your family controlling the pharmacy"* — greenhouse 1, an institutional asset worked by a university apprentice, on the university's inventory. He removes Nadia from that. He cannot remove flowerpots from a residential roof, and it would not occur to him to try. **This is the blind spot already banked above** (the *Fleming is trained to see coercive assets and not trained to see mead* note): planters are the same category as jam, cake and mead — invisible because they are not on a form.

**The split is precise, and it is what makes it work: the institution keeps the volume, the family keeps the potency.** Analgesia at hospital scale needs poppy by the plot — acreage, drying space, a supply chain, an apprentice. A lethal dose needs four plants in pots. *Atropa belladonna* and *Digitalis purpurea* are compact and a few of each is an arsenal. **So Fleming took the pharmacy and left the weapon, because the weapon is four flowerpots and a watering can.**

**And it re-answers the cooked-versus-good-deal question one more time, in her favour.** The green candies and the white ones in Maddy's satchel (`10-wild-oats.md:267`) never needed greenhouse 1. They come from home.

**Natural cap on scale, already motivated by the text:** `09-ground.md`'s whole storeroom argument is a load problem — honey too heavy for the upper floors, the larder driven down to the ground-floor common room. Planters full of wet soil are heavy for the same reason. So the roof supports a scatter of pots and not a garden, which caps the operation at exactly the size that keeps it beneath notice. **The building's own structural weakness is why the poisons stay deniable.**

**Register note:** never let anyone call it an arsenal, a stock, or a supply. It is pots on a roof, tended between the laundry and the clothesline, and nobody in the household remarks on it any more than they remark on the washing.

## Long-distance goods flow, from the Palaeolithic model — 2026-09-05

**Source:** Golovanova, Doronichev, Doronicheva, Sapega & Shackley 2021, *Long-distance contacts and social networks of the Upper Palaeolithic humans in the North-Western Caucasus (Mezmaiskaya Cave)*, J. Archaeol. Sci.: Reports 39:103118. In-repo at [`../research/sources/golovanova-2021-palaeolithic-long-distance-exchange.pdf`](../research/sources/golovanova-2021-palaeolithic-long-distance-exchange.pdf). A study of how foragers 40–24 ka moved obsidian, azurite, mammoth-tusk ornaments and Black Sea shells across hundreds of km. Terrace brought it for goods flow in the setting. Each finding below is anchored to a manuscript line; do not stack them.

**The paper's mechanism, in six moves:**

1. **Distance-decay.** Home range ≤100 km, preferred sources 30–40 km. Exotic obsidian is rare — 0.5–3% of lithics. The further the source, the rarer the good.
2. **Form encodes distance.** Obsidian arrives "ready-to-use small laminar blanks" or finished tools, only rarely as raw cores. You carry concentrated value over distance, never bulk.
3. **Maintained network vs sporadic contact.** Recurrent procurement from two *specific* sources across 15,000 years = "well-established social networks" — structured, directional, culturally bonded. Mammoth-tusk ornaments from the Russian Plain = rare one-off contact with "culture-unrelated" groups. Two different categories.
4. **Direct vs intermediary threshold.** ≤170–200 km one-way: "conceivably undertaken by the inhabitants themselves." >250–350 km: "some form of intermediary exchange." Past a walking threshold you stop controlling provenance.
5. **Non-utilitarian goods ride the utilitarian corridors.** Shells and the azurite pebble travel the *same paths* cut for obsidian, and so "outline directions and limits" of movement. The prestige object rides the necessity.
6. **Terrain gates the route; the network traces a cultural area.** They avoided the >3500 m passes; routes follow least-cost paths. The reach of a material outlines the boundary of a culturally-similar population; beyond it, contact is sporadic and with strangers.

**Implications for the setting — the load-bearing four:**

**A. The network-vs-sporadic split is what the naval pivot actually is.** `10-wild-oats.md` Weirwick is currently reached the sporadic way — the same register as `01-continuity-test.md:47`, *"another run by another crew to another town."* The Swartz pivot (cordage, pilotage, the cutter, marriage into Weirwick) is the move from sporadic contact to a **maintained, directional, kin-bonded network** — Golovanova's category 3. That is the difference between a trade and a dukedom: a one-off run does not build a polity, a recurrent socially-maintained corridor does. **The pivot's whole meaning is the category change, not the extra salmon.**

**B. Non-utilitarian rides utilitarian — Collie's method, named.** She already moves jam and mead (gift-register, non-utilitarian) down corridors cut for water, medicine and labour: `06-isolation.md:196` (jam sample to Bréal's woman) riding the pipe-repair emergency, `01-continuity-test.md:159` (cake to Bréal) riding the salvage convoy. The paper's finding is the general law under it: **the extent of Swartz social reach equals the extent of the Swartz material corridors.** Where the jam goes, the allegiance goes — and the pure-prestige objects to come (banknote, seal, heraldry) will ride the food corridors too. Symbolic goods map the reach of the network.

**C. "University country" is the direct-procurement radius; the Port is the intermediary zone.** The 3-day truck circuit, `~60 km/h` governed (`01-continuity-test.md:13`, `10-wild-oats.md:33`), defines the radius the university reaches *directly* — embedded, controlled, its people made "officers." This is Golovanova's ≤200 km direct band. `setting-map.dot`'s "beyond the horizon" Port cluster is the >250 km intermediary band: goods arrive light, finished, sporadic, and of **provenance the university cannot control.** The map already has the threshold drawn; the paper says what changes across it — control of provenance.

**D. The sea breaks the terrain gate.** Land routes are gated by fuel (`11-mulberry.md:113` "Joker on fuel"), road decay, and the mountain. A boat moves bulk cheaply where trucks cannot, so the naval pivot **converts far-and-sporadic coastal points into near-and-direct networked ones** — it extends the direct-procurement radius along the water. That is the mechanical reason the sea matters: Weirwick plus a cutter turns a stretch of coast into university country.

**Two texture rules that fall out, for prop plausibility (findings 1–2):**

- **Anything arriving from far must be light, finished and precious** — never bulk. Bulk calories come from near (butter, leather, jam from the fields, `02-fortification`/ch1). A working chip, a bottle of real wine, a watch — distant, finished, rare — are plausible imports; distant bulk is not. This disciplines what the Port can supply.
- **Form can signal origin.** Local salvage arrives raw and worked on site (Lewis's copper wire, `01`); distant goods arrive already concentrated (grapes are impossible, so *Château Swartz* is a local joke, `06-isolation.md:208`; finished wine could travel). Same logic as obsidian arriving as blanks, not cores.

**What NOT to import from the paper:** its foragers had no state, no currency, no coercion. The setting has all three emerging, which *inverts* one thing — Golovanova's networks were maintained by reciprocity and kinship alone, whereas the Swartz/Arbutus network is maintained by kinship **plus** the ratchet (necessity, standing, the banknote). So the setting's corridors are stickier and more directional than the Palaeolithic ones, and can be captured. That is the whole plot; the paper is the pre-state baseline it departs from.

---

## Temperate rubber — a crop the university would kill for — 2026-09-06

**Real, and it has a war behind it.** Natural rubber comes from *Hevea brasiliensis*, which is tropical and therefore gone. But two temperate species yield usable latex, and both were cultivated at scale precisely when tropical supply was cut: **Russian dandelion** (*Taraxacum kok-saghyz*, "TKS"), grown by the USSR from the 1930s and by the US and UK during the Second World War, and **guayule** (*Parthenium argentatum*), a Southwestern shrub the US ran an Emergency Rubber Project on from 1942. TKS is the better fit here — it is a **temperate herbaceous annual/short-lived perennial**, not a desert shrub, and it grows on ordinary ground in a cool wet climate.

**Why the university needs it more than it needs tyres.** Rubber in this setting is salvage and it is priced like a drug: `01-continuity-test.md:15` has rubber gloves costing **two vials of poppy**, with the manager noting *"the trucks will be going elsewhere next month."* The stock is finite and the good is consumable. What actually fails without it is **gaskets, seals, hose, tubing, diaphragms and gloves** — which is to say the water network (`06-isolation.md`), the clinic, and the laundry machines Alex keeps alive. **A polity that governs by holding a water contract cannot re-seal a single joint.** Salvaged rubber perishes; silicone does not, but nobody can make silicone. So the rubber curve is a slow, certain, unfixable decline unless somebody grows it.

**It belongs to Nadia, and it fits what she already is.** Her banked arc is landrace breeding and multi-generational selection (`10-wild-oats.md:203`, the new landrace easier on the land for proportionally less yield; the queen-rearing scene). TKS is a **breeding problem** — wild TKS yields poorly and the historical programmes foundered on exactly that, so it wants years of selection by someone with a nursery and patience. That is her job description. It also **pairs with hemp** rather than competing: hemp gives cordage, canvas and oakum, TKS gives the seals and hose. Fibre and gasket, one grower.

**And it complicates the naval pivot in a useful direction.** If the Swartzes hold cordage *and* the only domestic rubber, the house supplies both halves of what a boat and a pipe network need. That is a much harder thing for Fleming to reassign than a greenhouse bench.

**Open:** whether TKS is already in the agronomy programme or is Nadia's own proposal. The `06-isolation.md` greenhouse telegram lists the medicinal stock and no industrial crop, which weakly suggests the university has not thought of it. **Terrace's call**, and the second reading is better: the institution guards the pharmacy and misses the gasket.
